ParlaMint-AT_1997-09-19-020-XX-NRSITZ-00085_d7e2859	Thank you, Minister. We're coming to the 2nd. Question No 30, by Mr Paul Kiss: Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-09-19-020-XX-NRSITZ-00085_d7e2882	Federal Minister! My question is: [[/PAKT/VHG/XX/M/M_00153/index.shtml -- 153/M]] What measures do you take to ensure a national helicopter rescue service in the future?
ParlaMint-AT_1997-09-19-020-XX-NRSITZ-00085_d7e2910	Please have a reply.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-09-19-020-XX-NRSITZ-00085_d7e2925	Ladies and gentlemen! The helicopter rescue service is a very important facility for me. This institution has been established by law since 1982 and is supported by excellent cooperation between the countries, the federal government, especially the Ministry of Interior, partly also the Ministry of Defence, and the ÖAMTC. In recent years, there has been an unfortunate development, which has led to the fact that the helicopter rescue service has become very deficient and the loads have been carried almost exclusively by the Federal Ministry of the Interior. At the same time, we have to deal with the problem of having two-engine helicopters in operation due to new regulations. One of these two-engine helicopters will cost the 40 million schilling, so that in the case of a re-purchasing of ten such helicopters, investment costs of around 400 million schilling are necessary. And that is why I have started discussions with all those concerned, with the aim of achieving that the first part of the operation will be covered in some way and that, secondly, the new purchases will also be fairly divided between the individual participants. There is agreement between the Minister of the Interior and the countries that the continuation of this proven system will continue in the future and that public authorities should participate in this proven system. We have discussed a number of measures with a view to implementing these measures, such as, for example, getting higher cost contributions from social security companies and the General Insurance Institute, adopting a law that provides a legal basis for private insurance to take over the costs of helicopter rescue, and finally we want to achieve a fair balance of costs between the federal government and countries. I am convinced that I will be able to present a final report to Parliament this year on these measures.

We have a ground-bound, ground-station system, and there is an emergency medical system in each administrative district of Austria with the appropriate emergency doctors, with the appropriate technical equipment and also with duly well-trained emergency doctors. Helicopter rescue has the task of being a necessary and important complement to this ground-bound system, not only in difficult alpine terrain, but also in other areas. Because helicopter rescue has become highly deficient in recent years, it has been said by the then Minister of Finance and current Federal Chancellor that there is a need to cover costs here. There are different variants of how this cost coverage can be achieved. I have presented here today my ideas that we are trying to implement together with the countries. This requires support from the Minister of Finance, and I am also talking about it with the Ministry of Finance. Personally, I believe that one must be aware that a private person also has its costs for the rescue of helicopters and that, especially in the field of health, in the field of human salvation, it is a necessity that not profit is in the foreground, but aid to people. That is why I tend to believe that this necessary and important rescue will continue to be carried out by the public authorities in the future. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] One thing I have to say clearly – one has to be aware of –: Although the Ministry of the Interior would no longer carry out the rescue of helicopters, we would still have very high costs, since the Ministry of the Interior, especially in the area of helicopters, also has to perform other tasks, such as border service and traffic monitoring. That's why we need helicopters in the future. This means that, if the helicopter rescue were to be extended, a cost saving would not be given to the extent that many of it imagine.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-09-19-020-XX-NRSITZ-00085_d7e3797	Federal Minister! My question is: [[/PAKT/VHG/XX/M/M_00155/index.shtml -- 155/M]] How will the support for Bosnian refugees continue in the future?
ParlaMint-AT_1997-09-19-020-XX-NRSITZ-00085_d7e3824	I'm asking for a reply.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-09-19-020-XX-NRSITZ-00085_d7e3842	Deputies! I would like to reply briefly, but let me say at the same time that Austria has done a great deal of work on the support of the Bosnian refugees of war, which is recognised by all, including the UNHCR, so that Austria has a model effect on many other countries. We have received 95 000 Bosnian refugees, 60 000 of whom are integrated into Austria, with work and residence permits. Around 10,000 to 13 000 Bosnian war refugees have returned to their homes, 7 000 to 8 000 have moved on to other countries, and at present we do not have all 7 000 Bosnian war refugees still in the federal/country action. This federal/country action is at 31. It was limited in July 1998. Until then, these Bosnian war refugees will be cared for and supported by us. We have produced a catalogue of criteria, which I believe are very broad. My declared goal is to work together with the countries for these nearly 7 000 Bosnian refugees until 31. In July 1998, a definitive humane and reasonable solution was found. What will this look like? – Some of the Bosnian war refugees may return to their home country, which I think is right and good. However, most of them will not be able to do this because many are either sick or in need of care or come from an area where they are now ethnically in the minority. These people must, as far as possible, be integrated into the working world in Austria. Those in whom it is not possible for reasons of age or disease must be granted a permanent stay in Austria for humanitarian reasons. The talks with the countries are going well, and I am convinced that we will succeed.

Since the beginning of April this year alone, almost 1,600 persons have received a financial return aid of around 9 000 S per person. We have supported and supported a number of return aid projects. It has been appointed in Sarajevo for two years as a return agent of the Ministry of the Interior, whose task is to coordinate return on-site and to implement close cooperation with the non-governmental organizations, especially Caritas and Volkshilfe, but also many other organisations. We have launched the project "100 Houses", by which apartments and houses in Bosnia, especially in Central Bosnia, are to be made inhabitable, and together with the country of Tyrol, Mr. Khol, the project "Bürglkopf". We have also tried to give Bosnian refugees additional vocational training so that they can be integrated in their home country. And we, together with the Federal Chancellor's Office, plan to build or rebuild an old home in Bosnia. – In my opinion, the Austrian State has done a lot on this issue.

That means nothing but that the State will continue to assume its responsibility in this area, but we want to use other organisational structures to meet this responsibility. It is, of course, also legitimate to question the administrative structures of culture from time to time. But this is not a question of demonstrating a turning away from any cultural content, but rather of questioning the existing structure of the administration for efficiency and persuasiveness. But it is important to discuss it because different opinions are to be applied. It is also necessary to make the existing advisory system a little more transparent. It is also important to align art with something international. We will promote the artists in their efforts to be at international performances. We will also invite international art to be present in Austria. I believe that it will be a very important factor to focus more on international attention. This will be a priority in the future, as will a focus on film and audiovisual industry. We face new challenges. It is necessary to have a width in order to reach the top, but it must also focus on width promotion, be divided at the different levels and a corresponding distribution of funding. These were thus promoted by 3.6 times higher than the World Disability Championship, not to talk about European Championships and so on. These are morals of these awarding practices! That is really no longer to be represented today! I believe that this is urgently necessary, and I also believe that the forced membership of associations in sport is highly antique and must be stopped as quickly as possible. In this sense, in the near future, we will put in place an initiative proposal which I hereby announce.

As controversial as the sport of disabled people is in some areas – whether it is health-enhancing or harmful to health, or whether it is also intended to play sports as a disabled person – is: Scientific results show that disability sports promote disabled people in their health and do not harm them. Not only do disabled sport promote health: It is also an important psychological component for people who suddenly get out of their daily lives and have to start again. The sport offers very, very many opportunities in this area to be able to again actively participate in life. In Austria, the sport of disabled persons is given such a lower priority that, when Olympic Games take place, organisations have to beg – in the case of private individuals, in the case of small companies – to be able to finance at least a uniform training suit, because it is not even ensured that our team can appear in a uniform clothing. There are 12,000 disabled athletes in Austria and 100 active athletes who appear in world rankings. These people have only the chance to continue their sport because they pay almost everything from their own resources. There are hardly any sponsors for the sport of disabled people, because it is not usual and publicly recognized, because one is still afraid to use disabled people as promoters. It does not make a beautiful picture when a severely disabled person is shown on an advertising poster of a company. That is, even in the area of sponsorship there are the biggest problems to get additional funds. Disabled people also do not necessarily want to be sold at any price in the sense of sponsorship, but need state resources. These appropriations must be committed and guaranteed. Austrian disabled sport is trying to do its work responsibly and in the interests of the disabled people who run sports. In addition to the athletes, there is also a need for appropriate organizational and assistant staff. In other words, the cost of disabled sport is much higher than in so-called non-inhibited sports. We disabled people – I would like to say, I do not have a sport – but there is hardly any chance to exercise our sport accordingly, because all the sports facilities of the federal government, all sports homes are almost as good as not accessible. It's a skewer run and a bedding walk if disabled people want to practice sports somewhere. I think this is a missing policy. This is also the case in the field of the culture of the disabled: People with disabilities have been invited to parliament; Politicians have painted with them for half a day. ParlaMint-AT_1997-09-19-020-XX-NRSITZ-00085_d7e10742	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! With today's urgent request, we want to meet the social democrats' obligation to inform the interested Austrian people in a hot and emotional discussion process on genetic technology. How does the Austrian Federal Government continue to proceed in the Causa of the flagrant ban on genetic engineering maize? What steps does the Austrian Federal Government take to maintain the ban on imports, but not only these, but also this High House? Ladies and gentlemen! We would like to provide comprehensive information and discuss this. But before I come to the questions that are the subject of the urgent, a few thoughts on the whole subject matter: Like every thing, I believe that the problem of genetic technology has two sides. There is a wide range of discussion, and I am convinced that it will also be strained in today's debate. A large number of authorities, public institutions, associations and associations, scientists and scientists, as well as six ministries in the Republic, have to deal with this issue. The bow can be spanned from the scientific consideration of the application of this technology in medicine, in agricultural production, in the processing and preparation of the products produced according to this technology to the consumer. Biotechnology and genetic technology, as many say, are a forward-looking technology, both in economic and technical terms. They have enormous potential for development. Some places are called these two technologies in a breath with computer technology, information technology, but also telecommunications. And since biotechnology is a relatively young and emerging science, international corporations expect this technology and its results to generate huge profits for their companies. I can quote from the "Frankfurter Allgemeine Zeitung": According to Ernst & Young, the speculation fever has been reflected in the fact that the stock market value of biotech companies has increased from 52 billion dollars in mid-1995 to 83 billion dollars in 1996. – End of the quote. In my opinion, this speculation fever is mainly stimulated by the biotech-pharmaceutical area. This area – and this will be confirmed to me by all those colleagues who have discussed with me in the Subcommittee of the Health Committee with the experts – is largely out of question and out of the discussion. Austria is also meeting its international reputation as a leading industrial country in this modern field of biotechnology. The companies operating in this sector are, on the one hand, large world companies and, on the other hand, numerous small and medium-sized enterprises. This means for 5 700 employees in the large companies and around 900 employees in the 49 smaller and medium-sized enterprises. Six out of nine international groups operate research in Austria, and six institutes at Austrian universities are researching genetic technology and biotechnology in this field. But there is – I mentioned that earlier – also the side of the medal. With over 1.2 million supporting signatures – at least 21.3 percent of Austrian electorate – the genetic engineering people's desire has reached the second most successful conclusion in the history of the Second Republic. This popular desire was organized and borne by a wide variety of environmental groups, animal protection organisations, church organisations, organic farming plants and associations and by numerous scientists. In the issue 9/1996 of the magazine "ÖMIG" – Austrian dairy and food industry – it is to be read that a survey by the AK Consumer Protection Division revealed that 82 percent of Austrians – I quote in turn – would not "buy any genetically modified foods". "97 percent demanded a clear identification of such products." – This, ladies and gentlemen, is a clear task for politics. I should also like to draw your attention to the fact that trade and trade chains in Austria have already responded. For example, read in the "Cash" – I quote –: "The managers of the Billa Group have for many years had an unmistakable sense of what the consumer thinks. Last example: 85 percent do not want genetically modified foods – there will be none at Billa." – End of the quote. But not only trade, but also the gastronomy have responded to this broad movement in the population. For example, the leading top gastronists of Austria have already joined forces to form the "Gastronomy Against Genetic Manipulation" platform. The Vienna top gastronomy Christian Wrenkh means – I quote it here – : "As a cook, I can't take advantage of my guests. But I am responsible for serving healthy food to their best knowledge and conscience. That I don't look at genetic engineering food is of course for me." – These statements and quotations prove that there is a need for action and information. Ladies and gentlemen! There is, however, another area that is especially dear to my heart, namely Austrian organic farming and Austrian organic farming. They have done a lot in recent years and made great efforts to meet consumer demands and to produce free of genetic engineering. After a few discussions, the EU has also expressed a clear majority in favour of a general and strict ban on genetic technology in the field of organic farming. This has been possible only because of the pressure and the corresponding information. And what works gratifyingly in organic farming for all concerned could be achieved in the food sector – with corresponding cooperation and joint effort and openness. The discussions in the Subcommittee of the Health Committee, which I have already mentioned, have shown the range of discussion and expert opinions, but also the need for information. Ladies and gentlemen, who have discussed this together with the experts, will confirm this today. As part of the negotiations on the Seeds Act, which is also an important area for agriculture, a resolution was adopted which will lead to a draft by the Federal Minister for Agriculture and Forestry in the coming weeks, where clear labelling of genetically modified seeds is expected to be the content of this proposal. – This too is a step in the right direction, a step towards greater security for the producer, the processor and the consumer. In addition, in the negotiations on the 1997 ÖPUL programme, we agreed that in the autumn of this year it will be started to give a very intensive advice on the issue of freedom of movement for agriculture, even though it was not easy for Federal Minister Molterer to agree. Ladies and gentlemen! In the autumn of this year, goods are expected to be listed as free from genetic engineering in the shelves of our stores and our commercial houses. In my opinion, we are also on the right path to the implementation of what the Austrian population expects from us. The question of the patenting of organisms, the patenting of life is also a very important one that we will have to clarify in this discussion process and in this context. This autumn will certainly be filled with many negotiating and discussion dates on this subject. Ladies and gentlemen! I believe that with my remarks, in which I have still not mentioned all the areas which include genetic technology and biotechnology, I have nevertheless taken a little note of the range and diversity of this subject and explained it: from the point of view of those who condemn and defy genetic technology to the point of view of those who strongly support it. But I also hope that I could bring you the contradiction with these few sentences. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! For these reasons, with this urgent request for information to the interested population, we want to show and support the active path of the Austrian Federal Government to the "Import Prohibition" in the context of the "Gentechnologie" issue, not only to the 1.2 million sub-producers of genetic engineering, but also to the far-reaching number of interested people in our country.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-09-19-020-XX-NRSITZ-00085_d7e10855	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! High house! Before I come to the concrete answers, I would like to say a few introductory sentences. Even before the approval of the Genmais – and it is about the Genmais today – there have already been very controversial positions at European level. I would like to say that to all those who may not remember that anymore. For example, a large proportion of the Member States and the European Parliament have expressed concerns about the approval of the genetically modified maize under discussion today. – Despite these concerns, the European Commission has now unfortunately approved the authorisation or submitted the corresponding recommendation to the so-called Article 21 Committee. In February of this year, Austria, together with Luxembourg and Italy, issued and decreed the ban on imports of the Genmais. However, I regret that last week the Commission did not consider our reasons for the ban on imports to be sufficient and presented a proposal for a decision. I very much regret this decision because I believe that in this case the Commission has taken a wrong decision. I am still convinced that the Genmais is linked to risks that justify an import ban on the protection of citizens, but also our environment, and I am therefore determined not to accept a negative Commission decision but to take appropriate steps. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Now on the specific questions: Question 1: What measures will be taken If you decide to set your place of business by the Article 21 Committee? I would like to inform you that, together with the Federal Minister for the Environment, Youth and Family, Mr Bartenstein, I once again expressed our concerns to the Member States in writing and asked them to support our attitude in the decision in the Article 21 Committee. I will also use my personal contacts with the Member States in order to achieve support for our position. This seems to me to be necessary and useful because some Member States had already taken different positions in advance than the Commission did. I have also, of course, commissioned, during this period, the examination of all legal possibilities, in particular of bringing an action against a possible negative decision by the Commission. On question 2: Will you come back to the Member States again? I have already briefly explained this in my reply to Question No 1. A memorandum was prepared by Mr Bartenstein and me, and this has already been sent by the Ministry of Foreign Affairs to the ministers of the EU Member States concerned. I very much hope that, in the Article 21 Committee, under Directive 90/220/EEC, we can achieve a positive vote for Austria. There a qualified majority would be needed to deal with the Council of Ministers once again the importance of this decision not only for Austria but also for Europe as a whole. On Question 3: Will you approach potential importers and ask them not to import Genmais? At the moment – and I ask you to pay very close attention to this, because that is the point that is going to come forward – the import ban is still valid in accordance with Regulation BGBl. II/45/1957. Should the Article 21 Committee not have a favourable vote in favour of Austria and the EU Commission should therefore adopt its decision to repeal the Austrian import ban, I will, of course, take the steps to be taken by potential importers in addition to the steps to be taken under item 4 of my reply and point out that, in view of the public opinion in Austria, but also with regard to the Austrian concerns about this product, I would first of all like to refrain from imports of this product, in particular for the purpose of further processing of food and feed. There were several conversations between potential importers and me during the summer. I have started these talks, and of course these discussions will continue. On Question 4: What steps will you take in the event of a negative decision by the EU Commission? Since a final decision by the Commission is likely to be expected only in November of this year even in the event of an arbitrary defeat in the Article 21 Committee, I will make great use of the remaining time for considering such steps. I'll go into it in detail in the following. In any event, I am determined to use all the legal options I have to offer to me against such a negative decision. One of these possibilities is also to bring an active action against the Commission for the annulment of its decision to the European Court of Justice. At this point it should be noted that the staff members of the Constitutional Service, together with the International Office of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, are already very intensively examining and preparing the legal conditions for a complaint to the European Court of Justice. – For your information, what such a claim could cost: I think that the Porto for the letter to the European Court of Justice will be able to afford the Republic of Austria! [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Austria has the possibility of bringing an action for annulment under Article 173 of the EC Treaty against the decision of the European Commission within two months, extended by 10 days of the postal deadline, to the European Court of Justice. As grounds for complaint, the infringement of the Treaty – absolutely unacceptable risks in terms of health and life of humans and animals, as well as protection of the environment – is considered, as well as a misuse of discretion by the European Commission in the procedure provided for in the Directive, for example, the assessment of various expert opinions. In this case, it must be assumed that the European Commission will, in its counter-judgment to the European Court of Justice, represent the legality of its decision. That's the active way, briefly described. At the latest in the event of a rejection by the European Court of Justice of this annulment in the following months, such an action should come, Austria should also have an infringement procedure. In accordance with Article 163 of the EC Treaty, a decision by the European Commission to initiate such a procedure is required by the majority of its members. It seems unlikely that the European Commission will start infringement proceedings at the same time as an action for annulment by Austria. If Austria does not bring an action and continues to maintain the ban on imports of this genetically modified maize, i.e. if the decision of the European Commission is passed, an infringement procedure under Article 169 or Article 170 of the EC Treaty against Austria is likely to occur. [[Abg. May. Schweitzer: This is cheaper for taxpayers, isn't it?]] – I don’t know if the postage fee is so serious, otherwise I would like to take it from my own pocket. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Question 5: What are the reasons for a negative decision? The reasons for a negative decision can be given in terms of content, but also formal reasons. In any event, it will be clear that, in its decision, the Commission did not sufficiently appreciate the existing scientific concerns of Austria with regard to existing antibiotic resistance to the adverse effects of widespread use of BT toxins. It will also be appropriate to point out that further scientific advice would have been sought on the forthcoming issues and that the possible legal scope of Directive 90/220/EEC for the prescribing of restrictive conditions and conditions for placing this product on the market has not been exploited. I would also like to remind you here: These and similar genetically modified maize varieties are authorised in the USA, but not as the Commission has now approved, but in the USA – and we know the position of the USA on genetic engineering – it is common practice that monitoring programmes are required in the case of approval, but have been completely ignored by the European Commission in Austria or in Europe. Question 6: Do you intend to impose an import ban on gentech raps? Since the Commission has assessed two genetically modified rape lines positively, although Austria has raised reasoned objections, particularly from an ecological point of view, I am considering, in agreement with the Federal Minister for the Environment, Youth and Family, taking the appropriate measures in accordance with Article 16 of Directive 90/220/EEC. However, such a measure may only take place after the final approval by the competent French authority. Finally, let me say: It is a very serious matter that Austria, because it has good reasons for its right to its Austrian and therefore European law, strives, and I think, not only the Austrian population, but the whole European Union, all Member States are watching our steps with great curiosity and are eager to proceed with the procedure. One thing must be clear: The health and the environment are so much at our heart that we must do everything we can to protect and preserve them. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-09-19-020-XX-NRSITZ-00085_d7e11506	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Today's urgent request deals with a certain variety of maize, for which an import ban has been issued, which is now quite hotly fought, namely BT maize from Novartis, Ciba-Geigy. Concomitant antibiotic pressure through any antibiotic treatment may result in resistance formation. – As far as the contents are concerned. Point First reading of the motion for a resolution 442/A by Mr Mag. Helmut Peterand comrades concerning a federal law amending the Commercial Code, the Income Tax Act 1988 and the Corporate Tax Act 1988 (EURO-Balance Act)
ParlaMint-AT_1997-09-19-020-XX-NRSITZ-00085_d7e12754	Now we're moving to the 3rd place. Agenda item. We are going into the debate. First of all, I would like to inform the applicant, Mr Mag. Peter, the word. Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-09-19-020-XX-NRSITZ-00085_d7e12781	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen of the House! Allow me to present to you the request of the Liberal Forum for a Euro-Balance Act at first reading. What's this about? – It concerns an economic, a financial and technical matter. In the foreseeable future, we will exchange schilling against the euro. It will not be a currency reform, as is always claimed, but it will be a currency exchange. In As a result of this exchange of currencies, there is the possibility that we will also be able to do so through the IV. Council Directive of 25 December 1990 on the approximation of the laws of the Member States relating to the approximation of the laws of the Member States relating to the laws of the Member States relating to the laws of the Member States relating to the Member States relating to the laws of the Member States relating to the Member States relating to In this context, there is the possibility to reassess the business values in the balance sheets of Austrian companies. The few large companies in Austria have long solved this question by means of box constructions, holding companies and related re-evaluations. This possibility has so far been closed to small and medium-sized enterprises for cost reasons. However, it is particularly important in a single currency area, in which we want to benefit from the financial institutions together and make balance sheets comparable, that the balance sheets of good and healthy Austrian companies, which have been deformed by Austrian tax law for decades, can be updated on a voluntary basis. This prohibits today the Commercial Code, which lays down, in accordance with the strict low-value principle, that the purchase values or, where written down, the remaining book values may always be set. This leads, for example, to the fact that land 20 or 30 years ago incorporated into the company's assets appears in the books at values that do not have any relationship with the traffic values. As a result, there is an equity situation in Austria at book values, which is really dramatic. If you are going to a foreign bank with the Austrian balance sheet of a small or medium-sized business – which should also be an effect of the euro – then you have to take a kilogram of assessment reports that prove that the book values of the assets in your balance sheet are not at all correct, but that the real values can only be seen from the assessment reports. Foreign bankers cannot read our balance sheets because they do not have the necessary Austrian glasses. I therefore believe that the aim of our request should be to allow undertakings to carry out upgrades, both in terms of the immovable assets – land and buildings – as well as in terms of the movable assets: up to the maximum of the traffic values at ground and ground and up to the maximum of the acquisition values in the case of immovable property – building – and movable assets. Of course, if it is not a matter of land and land, it must be taxed; this tax represents a gain in recovery in the company's balance sheet. We propose to do this in concrete terms: in the case of moving assets, 15 percent, in the case of unmovable assets – building – 10 percent. In the case of unmovable assets, which cannot be depreciated – i.e. land – no special recovery tax can be lifted either. Of course, a revaluable reserve must be created for the upgraded immovable property in order to ensure that the capital collected in the accounts is not immediately removed from the company, for that would not make sense and aim of this measure. With this measure we are in the tradition of the Schilling opening up balance sheet, which took place 40 years ago and which had a very positive effect on the balance sheet structure of Austrian companies. We believe that this must be a voluntary measure, because it will be different between companies whether to take this step or not. We also believe that it is necessary to decide as an entrepreneur: If you carry out this upgrading, it takes place either for the entire immovable property – property –, the entire immovable property – building – or/and the entire movable property. I can tell you, on the basis of discussions with many of the economic fidelities with which I have been able to discuss this issue and which are very positive to the proposal, that the seven main reasons why such a measure is useful and that the exchange of Schilling against Euro is currently offering itself the exchange of currencies. It is an improvement in accounting undercapitalisation, leads to more balance sheet truth, more balance sheet clarity, and thus follows the business orders. We can thus represent the equity that will help healthy companies – it will not be useful for all companies – to escape a reorganisation process under the Company Reorganisation Act and the Insolvency Change Act. You know: If medium-sized enterprises have less than 8 percent equity from a certain size or a theoretical maturity longer than 15 years, they must start a reorganisation process. As I said, we are better comparable with the balance sheets in the EU area, and so we have the opportunity to build up successive investment reserves. It would also be a solution to the taxing of profits which one is now trying to get rid of over many, many bottlenecks, and I do not want to say here that I think that the investment exemption in the form we are currently having is no longer the instrument that we have believed to be able to find in it. In my opinion, this should be discussed. We also have a higher disposition capacity of companies if we leave the company with the choice of whether or not they want to take this measure. And, unfortunately, the Minister of Finance is not here now, of course, there is a priority for the Minister of Finance to receive more revenue, because these upgrades lead to tax benefits for companies, which can only be earned by companies by a factor of four or five years through higher depreciation. Ladies and gentlemen! I would ask you to consider this request seriously. I hope that we will soon have the opportunity to start negotiations in the Financial Committee and show you another step that would be taken with this request. There is a long discussion – including in the Ministry of Finance – about what we should do with the non-absorbed profits, especially with the unabsorbed profits of the individual companies. You know: The unclaimed profits of a capital company are taxed with 34 per cent corporate tax, the unabsorbed profits of a single company or a person company are subject to the 50 per cent limit rate of income tax. Therefore, it would be a good model to say that one takes the financial equity of a company, for example it pays off with the secondary market return, and this interest rate yield, which would be considered to be a kind of advance profit, is taxed only with the minimum KEST of 25 percent. It is not to be seen that someone who is setting up a company and his capital, which he had previously freely available and which he had been looking for, for example, in government papers, if you want, – there he paid the 25 percent KEST – is in the company, now on the 50 percent limit rate. This is a taxation of the substance of a person company or individual company, prevents the accumulation of capital for future investments and leads to ongoing capital outflows, because it is obviously more interesting to invest its money outside the company and pay 25 percent Kest than to leave it in the company where you pay 50 percent income tax for income from that capital. I believe that this is another contribution that we should be discussing in assessing solutions to corporate taxation. I therefore call for a serious and objective discussion. I should like to thank you in advance and hope that we will soon be talking seriously about these issues in the Committee. Time's pushing. - Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-09-19-020-XX-NRSITZ-00085_d7e12870	The next speaker is Mr Gusenbauer. Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-09-19-020-XX-NRSITZ-00085_d7e12891	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! I would like to deal with the questions raised at a first reading, as it says, without prejudging the discussions of the Committee on Budgets. According to the information I have received from the Ministry of Justice, this request appears to pose a problem with EU law with regard to the proposed amendment of the Trade Code. The evaluation provided for in this Directive is not yet compatible with EU law, at least after the Ministry of Justice has been informed, so an Austrian change could not be made without a specific EU legislation. In my opinion, there is also no change within the European Union in this direction, i.e. a trade-balance-sheet appreciation option up to the transport value. On the other hand, as far as the tax balance is concerned, it would, of course, be permissible to upgrade to the EU's transport values. I just wonder if it would bring about a real administrative simplification in the accounts of companies or whether the balance of trade and the tax balances of accounts would lead to a complete disparity between the commercial balance sheets and the tax balances. [[Abg. Böhacker: Mr. Böhacker, this is already the case! Trade law and tax law are already far apart!]] Right! But this would bring, at least in my understanding, an additional change. [[Intrusion of the Abg. May. Peter.]] What about EU compatibility? There should obviously be different views between you and the Ministry of Justice. As far as the question of the taxation of the whole is concerned, of course, it is attractive that you are looking for higher revenue. That sounds good. However, there will be fewer revenue in subsequent years due to correspondingly higher amortizations. [[Abg. Böhacker: Right!]] If we know that the linear rate of 34 per cent is applied to bodies and you only want to tax the recovery profits by about 10 to 15 per cent, then this means, of course, less revenue for the budget in the long term. That can be quite willful. However, I do not think that this is a very good proposal, at least at the hour. The third point that seems to me problematic is the question of the taxation of recovery profits. In fact, the mere setting of a tax rate for the recovery profit leaves the question open, such as in the case of losses arising from current business operations, losses arising from other types of income or losses. Should, for example, I imagine, an object tax-like recording of the recovery profit be done or should a positive income at least remain to the extent of the recovery profit in order to apply the tax rate in question? That is to say, even these administrative technical issues seem to me to be still in need of information. I therefore say that, for reasons of non-compliance with EU law, for reasons of reduced budgetary revenue, and for reasons of some technical inadequacy, I do not seem to be able to agree to this request at least at the hour. But we are going to discuss the question in detail in the Committee on Finance. - Thank you.

Vienna: 17.2 percent resolution rate, of which 33 percent, i.e. one third of the resolutions, in the trial period. So it turns out: There's enough room to see if it's going on or if you should stop. I therefore believe that the probationary period is sufficient. We don't need any special action. But there are also cases that sometimes cause us some concern. One of these I would like to briefly tell you: A so-called "special time" has been agreed. A girl is looking for a hairdressing school in Vienna, finds a teaching firm in the inner city, and the boss says: ==References== Start August of this year. She's happy to have solved her problem, quit school, and wants to finish in the month before the 5. Do their last vacation on August. There she gets a call from the company: We need you right away. Do you have time? Are you there? Don't you want to help out and cooperate before? – The girl says: Yes, of course, why not? How many times do you need me? You agree on three times a week. She completes this three times a week. One day or two days before the 5th. On August 15, the boss told the girl: I've been thinking about it. I don't really need an apprentice. And that's what's done. The girl now stands, goes to the employment office and may still hear the accusation: Now you're coming? Didn't you know that before? The question of the payment of this season is: There's no one. You were washed and cut twice free of charge! Such cases are now also pending in court. [[Abg. Dr. Drinkl: What a level! So there's block lessons now. The result: Since we have been teaching the block, the number of apprenticeships, especially the beginner, has fallen this year. "Block lessons, then more teaching places" was the promise, now we have block lessons and fewer teaching places. That's the experience we had to do. It can be seen that, despite the fact that we have fewer teaching posts for those seeking teaching, we have fulfilled the desire, whose implementation has always been signaled to us by the economy as very important. So we are halving, as I mean, enough time, but also enough work in front of us to make meaningful changes in vocational training law in the future. We should not say, especially in apprenticeship training, that the stupid is guilty of ourselves, but we should consider how we can also help the stupid in the sense of Mr Schweitzer. My group will continue to follow this path, and those who are willing to work towards improving apprenticeships, improving vocational training in the interest of our youth will always be open to us.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-09-19-020-XX-NRSITZ-00085_d7e14314	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! Austria is a member of the International Telecommunications Union, whose legal basis is the International Telecommunications Treaty. It regulates international cooperation in the field of telecommunications on a global basis. The International Telecommunications Union was founded in 1865 as a world telegraph association, has been a special organization of the United Nations since 1947 and is the oldest intergovernmental organization. It is based in Geneva and currently numbers 184 member states. Its objective is to facilitate peaceful relations and cooperation between peoples through a powerful telecommunications service. The International Telecommunications Treaty was revised in Geneva in 1992. The amendments to the Statutes and Treaties adopted in Kyoto in 1994 are to be put into effect for Austria. The Federal Ministry of Science and Transport has been authorized to carry out the contract. The Treaty therefore requires approval by the National Council. Ladies and gentlemen! In order not to lose the right to vote at the end of October 1997, I would ask you to give your consent to this State Treaty.
